virsh(1)

Paquete: libvirt

Licencias: LGPL-2.1-or-later, GPL-3.0-or-later

virsh(1) Soporte de Virtualización virsh(1)

Nombre

virsh - interfaz de usuario de gestión

SINOPSIS

virsh [OPCIÓN]... [CADENA_COMANDO]

virsh [OPCIÓN]... COMANDO [ARG]...

DESCRIPCIÓN

El programa virsh es la interfaz principal para gestionar dominios de invitados virsh. El programa se puede usar para crear, pausar y apagar dominios. También se puede usar para listar dominios actuales. Libvirt es un conjunto de herramientas C para interactuar con las capacidades de virtualización de versiones recientes de Linux (y otros sistemas operativos). Es software libre disponible bajo la Licencia Pública General Reducida de GNU. La virtualización del sistema operativo Linux significa la capacidad de ejecutar múltiples instancias de sistemas operativos concurrentemente en un único sistema de hardware donde los recursos básicos son controlados por una instancia de Linux. La biblioteca tiene como objetivo proporcionar una API C estable a largo plazo. Actualmente soporta Xen, QEMU, KVM, LXC, OpenVZ, VirtualBox y VMware ESX.

La estructura básica de la mayoría de los usos de virsh es:

virsh [OPCIÓN]... <comando> <dominio> [ARG]...

Donde comando es uno de los comandos listados a continuación; dominio es el ID numérico del dominio, o el nombre del dominio, o el UUID del dominio; y ARG son opciones específicas del comando. Hay algunas excepciones a esta regla en los casos en que el comando en cuestión actúa sobre todos los dominios, la máquina completa, o directamente sobre el hipervisor xen. Esas excepciones serán claras para cada uno de esos comandos. Nota: está permitido dar nombres numéricos a los dominios, sin embargo, hacerlo resultará en un dominio que solo puede ser identificado por ID de dominio. En otras palabras, si se proporciona un valor numérico, se interpretará como un ID de dominio, no como un nombre. Cualquier comando que comience con # se trata como un comentario y se ignora silenciosamente; todos los demás comandos no reconocidos se diagnostican.

El programa virsh se puede usar para ejecutar un COMANDO dando el comando y sus argumentos en la línea de comandos del shell, o una CADENA_COMANDO que es un único argumento del shell que consiste en múltiples acciones de COMANDO y sus argumentos unidos por espacios y separados por punto y coma o saltos de línea entre comandos, donde los pares de barra invertida-salto de línea sin comillas se omiten. Dentro de CADENA_COMANDO, virsh entiende las mismas secuencias de escape simples, dobles y de barra invertida que el shell, aunque debe agregar otra capa de escape del shell al crear el único argumento del shell, y cualquier palabra que comience con # sin comillas inicia un comentario que termina en un salto de línea. Si no se proporciona ningún comando en la línea de comandos, virsh iniciará un intérprete mínimo esperando sus comandos, y el comando quit saldrá del programa.

El programa virsh entiende las siguientes OPCIONES.

-c, --connect URI

Conéctate a la URI especificada, como si fuera con el comando connect, en lugar de la conexión predeterminada.

-d, --debug NIVEL

Habilita mensajes de depuración en el NIVEL entero y superior. NIVEL puede variar de 0 a 4 (predeterminado). Consulte la documentación de la variable de entorno VIRSH_DEBUG a continuación para obtener la descripción de cada NIVEL.

•
-e, --escape cadena

Establece una secuencia de escape alternativa para el comando console. Por defecto, se usa el ^] de telnet. Los caracteres permitidos cuando se usa la notación de gorro son: carácter alfabético, @, [, ], , ^, _.

•
-h, --help

Ignora todos los demás argumentos y actúa como si se hubiera dado el comando help en su lugar.

•
-k, --keepalive-interval INTERVALO

Establece un INTERVALO (en segundos) para enviar mensajes de keepalive para verificar si la conexión con el servidor todavía está activa. Establecer el intervalo a 0 deshabilita el mecanismo de keepalive del cliente.

•
-K, --keepalive-count CONTADOR

Establece el número de veces que se puede enviar un mensaje de keepalive sin obtener respuesta del servidor sin marcar la conexión como muerta. No hay ningún efecto en esta configuración en caso de que el INTERVALO se establezca en 0.

•
-l, --log ARCHIVO

Registra detalles en el ARCHIVO.

•
-q, --quiet

Evita mensajes informativos adicionales.

•
-r, --readonly

Realiza la conexión inicial en modo de solo lectura, como si fuera con la opción --readonly del comando connect.

•
-t, --timing

Muestra información del tiempo transcurrido para cada comando.

•
--no-pkttyagent

No registra pkttyagent como agente de autenticación con el demonio del sistema polkit, incluso si virsh se ha iniciado desde una terminal.

•
-v, --version[=short]

Ignora todos los demás argumentos e imprime la versión de la biblioteca libvirt de la que proviene virsh.

•
-V, --version=long

Ignora todos los demás argumentos e imprime la versión de la biblioteca libvirt de la que proviene virsh y qué opciones y controladores están compilados.

NOTAS

La mayoría de las operaciones de virsh dependen de que la biblioteca libvirt pueda conectarse a un servicio libvirtd que ya esté en ejecución. Esto generalmente se puede hacer con el comando service libvirtd start.

La mayoría de los comandos de virsh requieren privilegios de root para ejecutarse debido a los canales de comunicación utilizados para hablar con el hipervisor. La ejecución como usuario no root devolverá un error.

La mayoría de los comandos de virsh actúan de forma síncrona, excepto quizás shutdown, setvcpus y setmem. En esos casos, el hecho de que el programa virsh haya regresado, puede que no signifique que la acción se haya completado y debe sondear periódicamente para detectar que el invitado ha completado la operación.

virsh se esfuerza por lograr la compatibilidad hacia atrás. Aunque el comando help solo enumera el uso preferido de un comando, si una versión anterior de virsh admitía una ortografía alternativa de un comando u opción (como --tunnelled en lugar de --tunneled), entonces los scripts que utilicen esa ortografía anterior continuarán funcionando.

Varios comandos de virsh toman un entero escalado opcionalmente; si no se proporciona escala, el valor predeterminado se enumera en el comando (por razones históricas, algunos comandos tienen como valor predeterminado bytes, mientras que otros comandos tienen como valor predeterminado kibibytes). Los siguientes sufijos, sin distinción entre mayúsculas y minúsculas, se pueden usar para seleccionar una escala específica:

b, byte  byte      1
KB       kilobyte  1,000
k, KiB   kibibyte  1,024
MB       megabyte  1,000,000
M, MiB   mebibyte  1,048,576
GB       gigabyte  1,000,000,000
G, GiB   gibibyte  1,073,741,824
TB       terabyte  1,000,000,000,000
T, TiB   tebibyte  1,099,511,627,776
PB       petabyte  1,000,000,000,000,000
P, PiB   pebibyte  1,125,899,906,842,624
EB       exabyte   1,000,000,000,000,000,000
E, EiB   exbibyte  1,152,921,504,606,846,976

COMANDOS GENÉRICOS

Los siguientes comandos son genéricos, es decir, no específicos de un dominio.

help

Sintaxis:

help [comando-o-grupo]

Esto enumera cada uno de los comandos de virsh. Cuando se usa sin opciones, se enumeran todos los comandos, uno por línea, agrupados en categorías relacionadas, mostrando la palabra clave para cada grupo.

Para mostrar solo comandos de un grupo específico, proporcione la palabra clave de ese grupo como opción. Por ejemplo:

Ejemplo 1:

virsh # help host
Host and Hypervisor (palabra clave 'host'):
    capabilities                   capabilities
    cpu-models                     show the CPU models for an architecture
    c

Este sitio no está asociado de ninguna forma con Arch Linux ni afiliado a este proyecto. El contenido es únicamente una traducción de las páginas del manual de Arch publicadas en man.archlinux.org.

connect (re)connect to hypervisor freecell NUMA free memory hostname print the hypervisor hostname qemu-attach Attach to existing QEMU process qemu-monitor-command QEMU Monitor Command qemu-agent-command QEMU Guest Agent Command sysinfo print the hypervisor sysinfo uri print the hypervisor canonical URI

Para mostrar información detallada de un comando específico, dale su nombre como opción en su lugar. Por ejemplo:

Ejemplo 2:

virsh # help list
  NOMBRE
    list - listar dominios
  SINOPSIS
    list [--inactive] [--all]
  DESCRIPCIÓN
    Devuelve la lista de dominios.
  OPCIONES
    --inactive       listar dominios inactivos
    --all            listar dominios inactivos y activos

quit, exit

Sintaxis:

quit
exit

salir de esta terminal interactiva

version

Sintaxis:

version [--daemon]

Imprimirá la información de la versión principal sobre lo que se ha compilado. Si se especifica --daemon, la versión del demonio libvirt se incluirá en la salida.

Ejemplo:

$ virsh version
Compilado contra la biblioteca: libvirt 1.2.3
Usando biblioteca: libvirt 1.2.3
Usando API: QEMU 1.2.3
Hipervisor en ejecución: QEMU 2.0.50
$ virsh version --daemon
Compilado contra la biblioteca: libvirt 1.2.3
Usando biblioteca: libvirt 1.2.3
Usando API: QEMU 1.2.3
Hipervisor en ejecución: QEMU 2.0.50
Ejecutándose contra el demonio: 1.2.6

cd

Sintaxis:

cd [directorio]

Cambiará el directorio actual a directorio. El directorio predeterminado para el comando cd es el directorio de inicio o, si no hay una variable HOME en el entorno, el directorio raíz.

pwd

Sintaxis:

pwd

Imprimirá el directorio actual.

connect

Sintaxis:

connect [URI] [--readonly]

(Re)Conectar al hipervisor. Cuando se inicia el shell, esto se ejecuta automáticamente con el parámetro URI solicitado por la opción -c en la línea de comandos. El parámetro URI especifica cómo conectarse al hipervisor. Los documentos de URI https://libvirt.org/uri.html listan los valores soportados, pero los más comunes son:

  • xen:///system

    esto se utiliza para conectarse al hipervisor Xen local

  • qemu:///system

    conectarse localmente como root al demonio que supervisa los dominios QEMU y KVM

  • qemu:///session

    conectarse localmente como un usuario normal a su propio conjunto de dominios QEMU y KVM

  • lxc:///system

    conectarse a un contenedor Linux local

Para encontrar la URI actualmente en uso, revise el comando uri documentado a continuación.

Para acceso remoto, consulte los documentos de URI https://libvirt.org/uri.html sobre cómo crear URIs. La opción --readonly permite la conexión de solo lectura.

uri

Sintaxis:

uri

Imprime la URI canónica del hipervisor, puede ser útil en modo shell.

hostname

Sintaxis:

hostname

Imprime el nombre de host del hipervisor.

sysinfo

Sintaxis:

sysinfo

Imprime la representación XML de la información del sistema del hipervisor, si está disponible.

nodeinfo

Sintaxis:

nodeinfo

Devuelve información básica sobre el nodo, como el número y tipo de CPU, y el tamaño de la memoria física.

Se desaconseja fuertemente el uso de este comando, ya que no se garantiza que la información proporcionada sea precisa en todas las plataformas de hardware.

El valor de Frecuencia de CPU simplemente refleja la velocidad a la que se está ejecutando actualmente la primera CPU de la máquina. Esta velocidad puede variar entre CPUs y cambia continuamente a medida que el sistema operativo anfitrión la regula.

La estructura de datos utilizada para obtener los datos no es extensible, por lo que solo admite información de topología de nodos/sockets/cores/threads globales (sockets/cores/threads por nodo NUMA). Si la CPU del host tiene agrupaciones adicionales (por ejemplo, dies, clusters, etc.) o la topología NUMA no es simétrica, la estructura de datos no puede representar fielmente el sistema. En tales casos, se informa una topología falsa (nodos = 1, sockets = 1, cores = número de cpus del host, threads = 1) que solo representa correctamente el número total de CPUs del host.

El reemplazo recomendado es usar el comando capabilities, que informa los datos (excepto la frecuencia) bajo la ruta XPath /capabilities/host/topology.

nodecpumap

Sintaxis:

nodecpumap [--pretty]

Muestra el número total de CPUs del nodo, el número de CPUs en línea y la lista de CPUs en línea.

Con --pretty, las CPUs en línea se imprimen como un rango en lugar de una lista.

nodecpustats

Sintaxis:

nodecpustats [cpu] [--percent]

Devuelve estadísticas de CPU del nodo. Si se especifica cpu, esto imprimirá solo las estadísticas de la CPU especificada. Si se especifica --percent, esto imprimirá el porcentaje de cada tipo de estadísticas de CPU durante 1 segundo.

nodememstats

Sintaxis:

nodememstats [cell]

Devuelve estadísticas de memoria del nodo. Si se especifica cell, esto imprimirá solo las estadísticas de la celda especificada.

nodesevinfo

Sintaxis:

nodesevinfo

Informa sobre las características de seguridad de lanzamiento AMD SEV para el nodo, si las hay. Parte de esta información también se informa en el documento XML de capacidades del dominio.

nodesuspend

Sintaxis:

nodesuspend [target] [duration]

Pone el nodo (máquina host) en un estado de suspensión de todo el sistema y programa la interrupción del reloj en tiempo real del nodo para reanudar el nodo después de que expire la duración especificada por duration. target especifica el estado al que se suspenderá el host, puede ser "mem" (suspender a RAM), "disk" (suspender a disco) o "hybrid" (suspender a RAM y disco). duration especifica la duración en segundos durante la cual el host debe ser suspendido, debe ser de al menos 60 segundos.

node-memory-tune

Sintaxis:

node-memory-tune [shm-pages-to-scan] [shm-sleep-millisecs] [shm-merge-across-nodes]

Permite mostrar o establecer los parámetros de memoria del nodo. shm-pages-to-scan se puede usar para establecer el número de páginas a escanear antes de que el servicio de memoria compartida se ponga a dormir; shm-sleep-millisecs se puede usar para establecer el número de milisegundos que el servicio de memoria compartida debe esperar antes del próximo escaneo; shm-merge-across-nodes especifica si las páginas de diferentes nodos NUMA se pueden fusionar. Cuando se establece en 0, solo se pueden fusionar páginas que residen físicamente en el área de memoria del mismo nodo NUMA. Cuando se establece en 1, se pueden fusionar páginas de todos los nodos. Por defecto es 1.

Nota: Actualmente, el "servicio de memoria compartida" solo se refiere a KSM (Kernel Samepage Merging).

capabilities

Sintaxis:

capabilities [--xpath EXPRESSION] [--wrap]

Imprime un documento XML que describe las capacidades del hipervisor al que estamos conectados actualmente. Esto incluye una sección sobre las capacidades del host en términos de CPU y características, y un conjunto de descripciones para cada tipo de invitado que puede ser virtualizado. Para una descripción más completa, consulte:

https://libvirt.org/formatcaps.html

El XML también muestra la información de topología NUMA si está disponible.

Si el argumento --xpath proporciona una expresión XPath, se evaluará contra el XML de salida y solo se imprimirán los nodos que coincidan. El comportamiento predeterminado es imprimir cada nodo coincidente como un documento independiente; sin embargo, para facilitar el procesamiento adicional, el argumento --wrap hará que el nodo coincidente se envuelva en un nodo raíz común.

domcapabilities

Sintaxis:

domcapabilities [virttype] [emulatorbin] [arch] [machine]
                [--xpath EXPRESSION] [--wrap]
                [--disable-deprecated-feat



SYNOPSIS


virsh [-c | --connect URI] COMMAND [OPTIONS]






DESCRIPTION


The virsh utility is a management interface for the
    virtual machine monitor. It allows a system administrator to manage the
    hypervisor, domains, storage, networks, etc. The utility uses the libvirt
    API to interact with the hypervisor. It is possible to interact with any
    hypervisor supported by libvirt.


The default connection URI is typically the default hypervisor
    installed on the system (e.g. QEMU/KVM, Xen, VMware ESXi, etc.). This can be
    overridden by specifying the -c or --connect option.


A list of all available commands can be seen by running
    virsh without any arguments.






COMMANDS




capabilities


Syntax:






capabilities [--xpath XPATH] [--wrap] [--disable-deprecated-features]






Print an XML document describing the domain capabilities for the
    hypervisor we are connected to using information either sourced from an
    existing domain or taken from the virsh capabilities output. This may
    be useful if you intend to create a new domain and are curious if for
    instance it could make use of VFIO by creating a domain for the hypervisor
    with a specific emulator and architecture.


Each hypervisor will have different requirements regarding which
    options are required and which are optional. A hypervisor can support
    providing a default value for any of the options.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type used.
    The value to be used is either from the 'type' attribute of the
    <domain/> top level element from the domain XML or the 'type'
    attribute found within each <guest/> element from the virsh
    capabilities output. The emulatorbin option specifies the path to
    the emulator. The value to be used is either the <emulator> element in
    the domain XML or the virsh capabilities output. The arch
    option specifies the architecture to be used for the domain. The value to be
    used is either the "arch" attribute from the domain's XML
    <os/> element and <type/> subelement or the "name"
    attribute of an <arch/> element from the virsh capabililites
    output. The machine specifies the machine type for the emulator. The
    value to be used is either the "machine" attribute from the
    domain's XML <os/> element and <type/> subelement or one from a
    list of machines from the virsh capabilities output for a specific
    architecture and domain type.


For the QEMU hypervisor, a virttype of either 'qemu' or
    'kvm' must be supplied along with either the emulatorbin or
    arch in order to generate output for the default machine.
    Supplying a machine value will generate output for the specific
    machine.


If the --xpath argument provides an XPath expression, it
    will be evaluated against the output XML and only those matching nodes will
    be printed. The default behaviour is to print each matching node as a
    standalone document, however, for ease of additional processing, the
    --wrap argument will cause the matching node to be wrapped in a
    common root node.


The --disable-deprecated-features argument will modify the
    contents of host-model CPU XML, updating the features list with any features
    flagged as deprecated for the CPU model by the hypervisor. These features
    will be paired with the "disable" policy.






pool-capabilities


Syntax:






pool-capabilities






Print an XML document describing the storage pool capabilities for
    the connected storage driver. This may be useful if you intend to create a
    new storage pool and need to know the available pool types and supported
    storage pool source and target volume formats as well as the required source
    elements to create the pool.






inject-nmi


Syntax:






inject-nmi domain






Inject NMI to the guest.






list


Syntax:






list [--inactive | --all]
     [--managed-save] [--title]
     { [--table] | --name | --id } [--uuid]
     [--persistent] [--transient]
     [--with-managed-save] [--without-managed-save]
     [--autostart] [--no-autostart]
     [--with-snapshot] [--without-snapshot]
     [--with-checkpoint] [--without-checkpoint]
     [--state-running] [--state-paused]
     [--state-shutoff] [--state-other]






Prints information about existing domains. If no options are
    specified it prints out information about running domains.


Example 1:


An example format for the list is as follows:






``virsh`` list
  Id    Name                           State
----------------------------------------------------
  0     Domain-0                       running
  2     fedora                         paused






Name is the name of the domain. ID the domain numeric id. State is
    the run state (see below).


STATES


The State field lists what state each domain is currently in. A
    domain can be in one of the following possible states:




    
  
  • running
    
    The domain is currently running on a CPU
    
  
    • 
  
  • idle
    
    The domain is idle, and not running or runnable. This can be
        caused because the domain is waiting on IO (a traditional wait state) or
        has gone to sleep because there was nothing else for it to do.
    
  
    • 
  
  • paused
    
    The domain has been paused, usually occurring through the
        administrator running virsh suspend. When in a paused state the
        domain will still consume allocated resources like memory, but will not
        be eligible for scheduling by the hypervisor.
    
  
    • 
  
  • in shutdown
    
    The domain is in the process of shutting down, i.e. the guest
        operating system has been notified and should be in the process of
        stopping its operations gracefully.
    
  
    • 
  
  • shut off
    
    The domain is not running. Usually this indicates the domain
        has been shut down completely, or has not been started.
    
  
    • 
  
  • crashed
    
    The domain has crashed, which is always a violent ending.
        Usually this state can only occur if the domain has been configured not
        to restart on crash.
    
  
    • 
  
  • pmsuspended
    
    The domain has been suspended by guest power management, e.g.
        entered into s3 state.
    
  
    • 





Normally only active domains are listed. To list inactive domains
    specify --inactive or --all to list both active and inactive
    domains.


Filtering


To further filter the list of domains you may specify one or more
    of filtering flags supported by the list command. These flags are
    grouped by function. Specifying one or more flags from a group enables the
    filter group. Note that some combinations of flags may yield no results.
    Supported filtering flags and groups:






Persistence


Flag --persistent is used to include persistent guests in
    the returned list. To include transient guests specify
  --transient.






Existence   of managed save image


To list domains having a managed save image specify flag
    --with-managed-save. For domains that don't have a managed save image
    specify --without-managed-save.






Domain   state


The following filter flags select a domain by its state:
    --state-running for running domains, --state-paused for paused
    domains, --state-shutoff for turned off domains and
    --state-other for all other states as a fallback.






Autostarting   domains


To list autostarting domains use the flag --autostart. To
    list domains with this feature disabled use --no-autostart.






Snapshot   existence


Domains that have snapshot images can be listed using flag
    --with-snapshot, domains without a snapshot
    --without-snapshot.






Checkpoint   existence


Domains that have checkpoints can be listed using flag
    --with-checkpoint, domains without a checkpoint
    --without-checkpoint.


When talking to older servers, this command is forced to use a
    series of API calls with an inherent race, where a domain might not be
    listed or might appear more than once if it changed state between calls
    while the list was being collected. Newer servers do not have this
  problem.


If --managed-save is specified, then domains that have
    managed save state (only possible if they are in the shut off state,
    so you need to specify --inactive or --all to actually list
    them) will instead show as saved in the listing. This flag is usable
    only with the default --table output. Note that this flag does not
    filter the list of domains.


If --name is specified, domain names are printed instead of
    the table formatted one per line. If --uuid is specified domain's
    UUID's are printed instead of names. If --id is specified then
    domain's ID's are printed indead of names. However, it is possible to
    combine --name, --uuid and --id to select only desired
    fields for printing. Flag --table specifies that the legacy
    table-formatted output should be used, but it is mutually exclusive with
    --name, and --id. This is the default and will be used if
    neither of --name, --uuid or --id is specified. If
    neither --name nor --uuid is specified, but --id is,
    then only active domains are listed, even with the --all parameter as
    otherwise the output would just contain bunch of lines with just -1.
    If --table is combined with --uuid, then domain uuid is
    printed as an extra column.


If --title is specified, then the short domain description
    (title) is printed in an extra column. This flag is usable only with the
    default --table output.


Example 2:






$ virsh list --title
  Id    Name        State      Title
 -------------------------------------------
  0     Domain-0    running    Mailserver 1
  2     fedora      paused










freecell


Syntax:






freecell [{ [--cellno] cellno | --all }]






Prints the available amount of memory on the machine or within a
    NUMA cell. The freecell command can provide one of three different displays
    of available memory on the machine dependin




freeallpages


Syntax:






freeallpages [ { --all | --cellno cellno } ] [--pagesize pagesize]






Muestra la memoria libre total en la máquina en bytes, KB, MB o GB
    dependiendo de la máquina. Si se especifica la opción --all,
    mostrará la memoria libre en cada celda NUMA y la memoria libre total en la
    máquina. Si se especifica --cellno seguido de un número de celda,
    mostrará la memoria libre para esa celda NUMA específica. Si se especifica
    --pagesize seguido de un tamaño de página, mostrará la memoria libre
    en páginas de ese tamaño para la celda especificada o para todas las celdas
    si se usa --all.






freepages


Sintaxis:






freepages [{ [--cellno] cellno [--pagesize] pagesize |     --all }]






Imprime la cantidad disponible de páginas dentro de una celda NUMA.
    cellno se refiere a la celda NUMA que te interesa. pagesize
    es un entero escalado (ver NOTAS arriba). Alternativamente, si se usa
    --all, se imprime información sobre cada combinación posible de celda
    NUMA y tamaño de página.






allocpages


Sintaxis:






allocpages [--pagesize] pagesize [--pagecount] pagecount [[--cellno] cellno] [--add] [--all]






Cambia el tamaño del grupo de páginas de pagesize en el
    host. Si se especifica --add, entonces se agregan pagecount
    páginas al grupo. Sin embargo, si --add no fue especificado,
    entonces pagecount se toma como el nuevo tamaño absoluto del grupo
    (esto puede ser utilizado para liberar algunas páginas y reducir el tamaño
    del grupo). El modificador cellno se puede usar para acotar la
    modificación a una sola celda NUMA del host. En el otro extremo del espectro
    está --all, que ejecuta la modificación en todas las celdas NUMA.






cpu-baseline


Sintaxis:






cpu-baseline ARCHIVO [--features] [--migratable]






Calcula la CPU base que será soportada por todas las CPUs del host
    dadas en <archivo>. (Ver el comando
    hypervisor-cpu-baseline para obtener una CPU que pueda ser
    proporcionada por un hipervisor específico). La lista de CPUs del host se
    construye extrayendo todos los elementos <cpu> del <archivo>.
    Por lo tanto, el <archivo> puede contener un conjunto de elementos
    <cpu> separados por saltos de línea o incluso un conjunto completo de
    elementos <capabilities> impresos por el comando
    capabilities. Si se especifica --features, entonces la
    descripción XML resultante incluirá explícitamente todas las características
    que componen la CPU; sin esta opción, las características que forman parte
    del modelo de CPU no se listarán en la descripción XML. Si se especifica
    --migratable, las características que bloquean la migración no se
    incluirán en la CPU resultante.






cpu-compare


Sintaxis:






cpu-compare ARCHIVO [--error] [--validate]






Compara la definición de CPU del archivo XML <archivo> con la
    CPU del host. (Ver el comando
    hypervisor-cpu-compare para comparar la definición de CPU con la CPU
    que un hipervisor específico puede proporcionar en el host). El archivo XML
    <archivo> puede contener la definición de CPU del host o del invitado.
    La definición de CPU del host es el elemento <cpu> y su contenido tal como
    lo imprime el comando capabilities. La definición de CPU del invitado
    es el elemento <cpu> y su contenido de la definición XML del dominio o
    la definición de CPU creada a partir del modelo de CPU del host encontrado
    en el XML de capacidades del dominio (impreso por el comando
    domcapabilities). Además del elemento <cpu> en sí, este comando
    acepta el XML completo del dominio, el XML de capacidades o el XML de
    capacidades del dominio que contiene la definición de CPU. Para más
    información sobre la definición de CPU del invitado ver:
    https://libvirt.org/formatdomain.html#elementsCPU
    https://libvirt.org/formatdomain.html#cpu-model-and-topology. Si
    se especifica --error, el comando devolverá un error cuando la CPU dada
    sea incompatible con la CPU del host y se imprimirá un mensaje que brinda más
    detalles sobre la incompatibilidad. Si se especifica --validate, valida
    el formato del documento XML contra un esquema RNG interno.






cpu-models


Sintaxis:






cpu-models arch






Imprime la lista de modelos de CPU conocidos por libvirt para la
    arquitectura especificada. Si un hipervisor específico puede crear un dominio
    que utiliza alguno de los modelos de CPU impresos es una pregunta separada que
    se puede responder consultando el XML de capacidades del dominio devuelto por
    el comando domcapabilities. Además, para algunas arquitecturas libvirt
    no conoce modelos de CPU y los modelos de CPU utilizables se limitan solo al
    hipervisor. Este comando imprimirá que todos los modelos de CPU son aceptados
    para estas arquitecturas y la lista real de modelos de CPU soportados se puede
    verificar en el XML de capacidades del dominio.






hypervisor-cpu-compare


Sintaxis:






hypervisor-cpu-compare ARCHIVO [virttype] [emulator] [arch] [machine] [--error] [--validate]






Compara la definición de CPU del archivo XML <archivo> con la CPU
    que el hipervisor puede proporcionar en el host. (Esto es diferente de
    cpu-compare, que compara la definición de CPU con la CPU del host sin
    considerar ningún hipervisor específico y sus capacidades).


El ARCHIVO XML debe contener una definición de CPU del
    invitado: ya sea el elemento <cpu> y su contenido de una definición
    XML de dominio o una definición de CPU creada a partir del modelo de CPU del
    host encontrado en el elemento <mode name="host-model">
    en el XML de capacidades del dominio (impreso por el comando
    domcapabilities). El elemento <mode name="host-model">
    en sí mismo o incluso su elemento padre <cpu> encontrado en el
    XML de capacidades del dominio no son aceptados. El elemento tiene que ser
    transformado en una definición de CPU real. Para más información sobre la
    definición de CPU del invitado ver:
    https://libvirt.org/formatdomain.html#elementsCPU
    https://libvirt.org/formatdomain.html#cpu-model-and-topology.


Alternativamente, este comando extraerá automáticamente la
    definición de CPU cuando se le proporcione un XML completo del dominio o de
    capacidades del dominio.


Por razones históricas, el ARCHIVO XML también puede contener
    una definición de CPU del host, pero tal uso se desaconseja firmemente ya que
    probablemente proporcionará resultados incorrectos.


La opción virttype especifica el tipo de virtualización
    (utilizable en el atributo 'type' del elemento de nivel superior <domain>
    del XML del dominio). emulator especifica la ruta al emulador,
    arch especifica la arquitectura de la CPU y machine especifica el
    tipo de máquina. Si se especifica --error, el comando devolverá un error
    cuando la CPU dada sea incompatible con la CPU del host y se imprimirá un
    mensaje proporcionando más detalles sobre la incompatibilidad. Si se
    especifica --validate, valida el formato del documento XML contra un
    esquema RNG interno.






hypervisor-cpu-baseline


Sintaxis:






hypervisor-cpu-baseline [ARCHIVO] [virttype] [emulator] [arch] [machine]
   [--features] [--migratable] [--ignore-host] [model]






Calcula una CPU base que será compatible con todas las CPUs definidas
    en un ARCHIVO XML. Este comando debe ser llamado en uno de los hosts
    descritos en ARCHIVO. Llamarlo en otro host resulta en un comportamiento
    indefinido ya que el modelo de CPU calculado está influenciado por el
    hipervisor (el resultado puede usar un modelo de CPU inesperado o algunas
    características pueden ser deshabilitadas aunque sean soportadas por todas las
    CPUs de entrada). El comportamiento indefinido puede ser evitado usando la
    opción --ignore-host (ver abajo).


Esto es diferente de cpu-baseline, que no considera ninguna
    capacidad del hipervisor al calcular la CPU base.


Como alternativa a ARCHIVO en caso de que el XML solo contenga
    un modelo de CPU sin características adicionales, se puede pasar el nombre del
    modelo de CPU en sí como model. Se debe usar exactamente uno entre
    ARCHIVO y model.


El ARCHIVO XML debe contener definiciones de CPU del invitado
    creadas a partir del modelo de CPU del host encontrado en los XML de
    capacidades del dominio del elemento <mode name="host-model">
    (impresos por el comando domcapabilities en cada host). Los elementos
    <mode name="host-model"> en sí mismos o incluso sus elementos
    padre <cpu> encontrados en los XML de capacidades del dominio no
    son aceptados. Los elementos tienen que ser transformados en definiciones de
    CPU reales.


Alternativamente, este comando extraerá automáticamente las
    definiciones de CPU cuando se le proporcionen XML de capacidades del dominio.


Por razones históricas, el ARCHIVO XML también puede contener
    definiciones de CPU del host, pero tal uso se desaconseja firmemente ya que
    probablemente proporcionará resultados incorrectos.


Cuando ARCHIVO contiene solo una definición de CPU, el comando
    imprimirá la misma CPU con las restricciones impuestas por las capacidades del
    hipervisor. Específicamente, ejecutar el comando virsh hypervisor-cpu-baseline
    sin opciones adicionales sobre el resultado de virsh domcapabilities
    transformará el modelo de CPU del host del XML de capacidades del dominio a una
    forma directamente utilizable en el XML del dominio. Ejecutar el comando con
    model (o ARCHIVO que contenga solo una definición de CPU con
    modelo y sin elementos de características) que esté marcada como no utilizable
    en virsh domcapabilities proporcionará una lista de características que
    impiden que este modelo de CPU sea utilizable.


La opción virttype especifica el tipo de virtualización
    (utilizable en el atributo 'type' del elemento de nivel superior <domain>
    del XML del dominio). emulator especifica la ruta al emulador,
    arch especifica la arquitectura de la CPU y machine especifica el
    tipo de máquina. Si se especifica --features, entonces la descripción
    XML resultante incluirá explícitamente todas las características que componen la
    CPU; sin esta opción, las características que forman parte del modelo de CPU no
    se listarán en la descripción XML. Si se especifica --migratable, las
    características que bloquean la migración no se incluirán en la CPU resultante.
    Si se especifica --ignore-host y ARCHIVO contiene más de una
    CPU, el comando no considerará las capacidades del hipervisor al calcular la
    base. Con esta opción, la base se puede calcular de forma segura en un






HYPERVISOR COMMANDS


These commands query and manipulate the hypervisor. This is typically
    QEMU, but can be any hypervisor that libvirt has been configured to use.




hypervisor-cpu-baseline


Syntax:






hypervisor-cpu-baseline [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine]






Print the CPU model for the specified architecture that is the
    most compatible baseline CPU model available in the hypervisor. This is
    useful for finding a CPU model that will be compatible across many different
    host systems.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.






hypervisor-cpu-compare


Syntax:






hypervisor-cpu-compare [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine] [--baseline]
   {domain | file:/path/to/domain.xml}






Compare the CPU model of a domain (or a domain XML file) with the
    CPU models available in the hypervisor. This command provides detailed
    information about the compatibility of the domain's CPU model with the
    host.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.


The --baseline option compares the domain's CPU model
    against the baseline CPU model reported by
    hypervisor-cpu-baseline.


The first parameter can be a domain name or a path to a domain XML
    file prefixed with file:. If no domain or file is given, the command
    compares against the CPU models of the hypervisor on the host.


The output indicates whether the domain's CPU model is fully
    compatible, partially compatible, or not compatible with the host's CPU
    features. It also lists any specific features that are available or missing.


This command is useful for troubleshooting CPU-related issues and
    for understanding the capabilities of virtual CPUs within the context of the
    host hardware and hypervisor configuration. It helps in determining if a
    domain will run correctly and perform optimally on the current host. It
    considers all CPUs of the host (even those not described in FILE).






hypervisor-cpu-models


Syntax:






hypervisor-cpu-models [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine] [--all]






Print the list of CPU models known by the hypervisor for the
    specified architecture. It is not guaranteed that a listed CPU will run on
    the host. To determine CPU model compatibility with the host, see
    virsh hypervisor-cpu-baseline and virsh hypervisor-cpu-compare.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.


By default, only the models that are claimed to be
    "usable" by the hypervisor on the host are reported. The option
    --all will report every CPU model known to the hypervisor, including
    ones that are not supported on the hypervisor (e.g. newer generation
    models).








DOMAIN COMMANDS


The following commands manipulate domains directly, as stated
    previously most commands take domain as the first parameter. The
    domain can be specified as a short integer, a name or a full
    UUID.




autostart


Syntax:






autostart [--disable] [--once] domain






Configure a domain to be automatically started at each boot of the
    host. The --once option configures the domain to be started on the
    next boot of the host.


The option --disable disables the corresponding
    autostarting.


Note that autostart configured via the --once option is
    independent from the autostart configured without it. Enabling either of
    them will cause the VM to be started on the next boot of the host.






blkdeviotune


Syntax:






blkdeviotune domain device [[--config] [--live] | [--current]]
   [[total-bytes-sec] | [read-bytes-sec] [write-bytes-sec]]
   [[total-iops-sec] | [read-iops-sec] [write-iops-sec]]
   [[total-bytes-sec-max] | [read-bytes-sec-max] [write-bytes-sec-max]]
   [[total-iops-sec-max] | [read-iops-sec-max] [write-iops-sec-max]]
   [[total-bytes-sec-max-length] |
    [read-bytes-sec-max-length] [write-bytes-sec-max-length]]
   [[total-iops-sec-max-length] |
    [read-iops-sec-max-length] [write-iops-sec-max-length]]
   [size-iops-sec] [group-name]






Set or query the block disk io parameters for a block device of
    domain. device specifies a unique target name (<target
    dev='name'/>) or source file (<source file='name'/>) for one of the
    disk devices attached to domain (see also domblklist for
    listing these names).


If no limit is specified, it will query current I/O limits
    setting. Otherwise, alter the limits with these flags:
    --total-bytes-sec specifies total throughput limit as a scaled
    integer, the default being bytes per second if no suffix is specified.
    --read-bytes-sec specifies read throughput limit as a scaled integer,
    the default being bytes per second if no suffix is specified.
    --write-bytes-sec specifies write throughput limit as a scaled
    integer, the default being bytes per second if no suffix is specified.
    --total-iops-sec specifies total I/O operations limit per second.
    --read-iops-sec specifies read I/O operations limit per second.
    --write-iops-sec specifies write I/O operations limit per second.
    --total-bytes-sec-max specifies maximum total throughput limit as a
    scaled integer, the default being bytes per second if no suffix is specified
    --read-bytes-sec-max specifies maximum read throughput limit as a
    scaled integer, the default being bytes per second if no suffix is
    specified. --write-bytes-sec-max specifies maximum write throughput
    limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes per second if no suffix
    is specified. --total-iops-sec-max specifies maximum total I/O
    operations limit per second. --read-iops-sec-max specifies maximum
    read I/O operations limit per second. --write-iops-sec-max specifies
    maximum write I/O operations limit per second.
    --total-bytes-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to allow
    maximum total throughput limit. --read-bytes-sec-max-length specifies
    duration in seconds to allow maximum read throughput limit.
    --write-bytes-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to allow
    maximum write throughput limit. --total-iops-sec-max-length specifies
    duration in seconds to allow maximum total I/O operations limit.
    --read-iops-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to allow
    maximum read I/O operations limit. --write-iops-sec-max-length
    specifies duration in seconds to allow maximum write I/O operations limit.
    --size-iops-sec specifies size I/O operations limit per second.
    --group-name specifies group name to share I/O quota between multiple
    drives. For a QEMU domain, if no name is provided, then the default is to
    have a single group for each device.


Older versions of virsh only accepted these options with
    underscore instead of dash, as in --total_bytes_sec.


Bytes and iops values are independent, but setting only one value
    (such as --read-bytes-sec) resets the other two in that category to
    unlimited. An explicit 0 also clears any limit. A non-zero value for a given
    total cannot be mixed with non-zero values for read or write.


It is up to the hypervisor to determine how to handle the length
    values. For the QEMU hypervisor, if an I/O limit value or maximum value is
    set, then the default value of 1 second will be displayed. Supplying a 0
    will reset the value back to the default.


If --live is specified, affect a running guest. If
    --config is specified, affect the next start of a persistent guest.
    If --current is specified, it is equivalent to either --live
    or --config, depending on the current state of the guest. When
    setting the disk io parameters both --live and --config flags
    may be given, but --current is exclusive. For querying only one of
    --live, --config or --current can be specified. If no
    flag is specified, behavior is different depending on hypervisor.






domthrottlegroupset


Syntax:






domthrottlegroupset domain group-name [[--config] [--live] | [--current]]
   [[total-bytes-sec] | [read-bytes-sec] [write-bytes-sec]]
   [[total-iops-sec] | [read-iops-sec] [write-iops-sec]]
   [[total-bytes-sec-max] | [read-bytes-sec-max] [write-bytes-sec-max]]
   [[total-iops-sec-max] | [read-iops-sec-max] [write-iops-sec-max]]
   [[total-bytes-sec-max-length] |
    [read-bytes-sec-max-length] [write-bytes-sec-max-length]]
   [[total-iops-sec-max-length] |
    [read-iops-sec-max-length] [write-iops-sec-max-length]]
   [size-iops-sec]






Add or update a throttle group against specific domain.
    group-name specifies a unique throttle group name, which defines
    limit, and will be referenced by drives.


If no limit is specified, default them as all zeros, which will
    fail, Otherwise, set limits with these flags: --total-bytes-sec
    specifies total throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being
    bytes per second if no suffix is specified. --read-bytes-sec
    specifies read throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes
    per second if no suffix is specified. --write-bytes-sec specifies
    write throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes per
    second if no suffix is specified. --total-iops-sec specifies total
    I/O operations limit per second. --read-iops-sec specifies read I/O
    operations limit per second. --write-iops-sec specifies write I/O
    operations limit per second. --total-bytes-sec-max specifies maximum
    total throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes per
    second if no suffix is specified --read-bytes-sec-max specifies
    maximum read throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes
    per second if no suffix is specified. --write-bytes-sec-max specifies
    maximum write throughput limit as a scaled integer, the default being bytes
    per second if no suffix is specified. --total-iops-sec-max specifies
    maximum total I/O operations limit per second. --read-iops-sec-max
    specifies maximum read I/O operations limit per second.
    --write-iops-sec-max specifies maximum write I/O operations limit per
    second. --total-bytes-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to
    allow maximum total throughput limit. --read-bytes-sec-max-length
    specifies duration in seconds to allow maximum read throughput limit.
    --write-bytes-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to allow
    maximum write throughput limit. --total-iops-sec-max-length specifies
    duration in seconds to allow maximum total I/O operations limit.
    --read-iops-sec-max-length specifies duration in seconds to allow
    maximum read I/O operations limit. --write-iops-sec-max-length
    specifies duration in seconds to allow maximum write I/O operations limit.
    --size-iops-sec specifies size I/O operations limit per second.


Bytes and iops values are independent, but setting only one value
    (such as --read-bytes-sec) resets the other two in that category to
    unlimited. An explicit 0 also clears any limit. A non-zero value for a given
    total cannot be mixed with non-zero values for read or write.


It is up to the hypervisor to determine how to handle the length
    values. For the QEMU hypervisor, if an I/O limit value or maximum value is
    set, then the default value of 1 second will be displayed. Supplying a 0
    will reset the value back to the default.


If --live is specified, affect a running guest. If
    --config is specified, affect the next start of a persistent guest.
    If --current is specified, it is equivalent to either --live
    or --config, depending on the current state of the guest. When
    setting the disk io parameters both --live and --config are
    specified, both live configuration and config are updated while setting the
    description, but --current is exclusive. If no flag is specified,
    behavior is different depending on hypervisor.






domthrottlegroupset


Syntax:






domthrottlegroupdel domain group-name [[--config] [--live] | [--current]]






Remove a throttle group definition from domain. Without any
    argument, it removes throttle group definition from both live guest and
    persistent configuration.






domblockadd


Syntax:






domblockadd domain device [alias] [--path path] [--format format]
   [--backing-path path] [--driver type] [--mode scsi] [--serial serial]
   [--tray-device tray] [--detect-devices]






Add a block device to a domain. The device specifies the
    target name for the new block device, e.g. vda or sda.
    The optional alias specifies an alias for the device. The
    --path option specifies the path to the block device (e.g. a disk
    image file, LVM volume, or raw device node). The --format option
    (e.g. qcow2) specifies the format of the disk image if creating
    a new disk image file. The --backing-path option specifies the path
    to a backing file for a disk image. The --driver option specifies the
    type of driver to use (e.g. qemu). The --mode option
    sets the SCSI mode (e.g. scsi). The --serial option
    sets the serial number. The --tray-device option specifies the tray
    device for CD-ROMs. The --detect-devices option attempts to
    automatically detect and add available host block devices.


This command modifies the domain's runtime configuration. To make
    the change persistent, use the --config flag along with the
    appropriate flags to ensure the device is correctly defined in the domain XML.
    If only runtime changes are desired, use the --live flag. Use
    --current to apply changes to both runtime and persistent
    configuration.






domblockcopy


Syntax:






domblockcopy domain disk source_image [--dest dest_image]
   [--reuse-dest] [--shallow] [--deep] [--check-dest no|auto|always]
   [--wait]






Copy a block device's contents from a source image to a destination
    image. domain is the domain name or ID. disk is the disk name
    as shown by domblklist. source_image specifies the
    source disk image path. --dest specifies the destination disk image
    path. If omitted, the destination will be a new image in the same directory
    as the source image with a modified name. --reuse-dest allows using
    an existing destination image. --shallow performs a shallow copy,
    only copying specified metadata. --deep performs a deep copy, copying
    all data. --check-dest controls pre-copy destination checking:
    no disables checking, auto performs limited
    checking, and always performs thorough checking.
    --wait makes the command block until the copy operation is complete.


This command is useful for migrating disks, creating backups, or
    preparing snapshots. It supports various copy modes to balance performance and
    completeness.






domblockdelete


Syntax:






domblockdelete domain device [--config] [--live] [--current]






Remove a block device from a domain. device specifies the
    target name of the block device to remove (e.g., vda). The
    options --config, --live, and --current specify
    whether to remove the device from the persistent configuration, the runtime
    configuration, or both.


Use this command to detach storage devices from a virtual machine.
    Note that this command only removes the device definition; it does not delete
    the underlying storage file or volume.






domblockjobabort


Syntax:






domblockjobabort domain disk [--pivot]






Abort a block device job (e.g., copy, backup, snapshot) for a given
    domain and disk. The --pivot option attempts to pivot
    the job to a new destination if one is available.


This command is useful for interrupting long-running storage
    operations that are no longer needed or have encountered errors.






domblockjobinfo


Syntax:






domblockjobinfo domain disk






Retrieve information about an ongoing block device job for a given
    domain and disk. This includes details like job type, progress,
    speed, and estimated time remaining.


This command is essential for monitoring the status of storage
    operations such as disk copying or snapshot creation.






domblockresize


Syntax:






domblockresize domain disk [--size size] [--path path] [--config]
   [--live] [--current]






Resize a block device for a domain. domain is the domain
    name or ID. disk is the disk name as shown by domblklist.
    The --size option specifies the new size in bytes (use K, M, G, T, P, E
    suffixes for KiB, MiB, GiB, etc.). The --path option is required for
    growing a virtual disk file and specifies the path to the disk image. The
    flags --config, --live, and --current determine if the
    resize operation applies to the persistent configuration, the running guest,
    or both.


This command allows for dynamic expansion of virtual disk sizes,
    provided the underlying storage format and hypervisor support it.






domblame


Syntax:






domblame domain






Print the name of the user who owns the specified domain.






domblkdevice


Syntax:






domblkdevice domain [--human]






List the block devices associated with a domain. The
    --human option displays sizes in human-readable format.






domblklist


Syntax:






domblklist domain [--human]






List all block devices used by a domain, showing target
    names, source paths, and storage formats. The --human option displays
    sizes in human-readable format.






dombootdevices


Syntax:






dombootdevices domain






List the boot devices for a domain. This shows the order in
    which the domain attempts to boot from different device types (e.g., cdrom,
    disk, network).






domcpu


Syntax:






domcpu domain [--live] [--current]






Show CPU configuration for a domain. The --live option
    shows the current live CPU configuration, while --current shows the
    configuration for the current boot.






domcpumodify


Syntax:






domcpumodify domain [--live] [--current] --count count
   [--sockets sockets] [--cores cores] [--threads threads]
   [--maxvcpus maxvcpus] [--emulatorpin vcpus] [--vcpupin N=M,...]






Modify the CPU configuration for a domain. The
    --count option sets the number of virtual CPUs. Other options allow
    fine-tuning of the CPU topology (sockets, cores, threads), maximum vCPUs,
    emulator pinning, and individual vCPU pinning to host CPUs. The
    --live and --current flags control whether changes apply to the
    running guest or the persistent configuration.


Use this command carefully, as incorrect CPU settings can impact
    performance or stability.






domcsvdevice


Syntax:






domcsvdevice domain [--all] [device]






List the host devices passed through to the domain. If
    device is specified, show details only for that device. The
    --all option lists all configured host devices.






domdump


Syntax:






domdump domain [--destpath path] [--crash]






Dump the memory contents of a running domain to a file. The
    --destpath option specifies the output file path. The --crash
    option indicates that this is a crash dump.


This is useful for debugging purposes, allowing post-mortem analysis
    of the domain's state.






domedit


Syntax:






domedit domain






Edit the XML configuration of a domain using the default text
    editor. Changes are applied immediately to the domain's configuration.


Use with caution, as invalid XML can cause domain start failures.






domguestcpuinfo


Syntax:






domguestcpuinfo domain






Show detailed CPU information for the guest operating system running
    inside the specified domain. This includes information about the guest's
    view of the CPUs, such as model, frequency, and features.






domiface


Syntax:






domiface domain [--human]






List the network interfaces attached to a domain. The
    --human option displays link/transfer speeds in human-readable format.






domifcreate


Syntax:






domifcreate domain bridge [script=/path/to/script]






Create and attach a network interface to a domain.
    bridge specifies the name of the network bridge on the host to connect
    the interface to. The optional script parameter can specify a custom
    script for interface configuration.


This command modifies the runtime configuration of the domain. To
    make the change persistent, edit the domain's XML configuration.






domifdefine


Syntax:






domifdefine domain iface-xml-file






Define a network interface for a domain based on the
    specified XML configuration file. This command adds the interface definition
    to the domain's persistent configuration.






domifdestroy


Syntax:






domifdestroy domain device






Destroy (detach) a network interface from a domain.
    device specifies the target name of the interface (e.g., vnet0).


This command modifies the runtime configuration of the domain. To
    make the change persistent, edit the domain's XML configuration.






domifinfo


Syntax:






domifinfo domain device






Display detailed information about a specific network interface
    (device) attached to a domain. This includes MAC address, IP
    addresses, traffic statistics, and connection details.






domiflist


Syntax:






domiflist domain [--human]






List network interfaces for a domain. Shows target name, type, MAC
    address, and the network it's connected to. The --human option
    displays traffic statistics in human-readable format.




Example


  virsh # domiflist win10
Interface  Type   Source   Model   MAC
-----------------------------------------------------
vnet0      bridge vnet0    virtio  52:54:00:89:ab:cd
vnet1      user   --       virtio  52:54:00:7e:ab:cd

  virsh # domiflist --human win10
Interface  Type   Source   Model   MAC       Bytes Rx      Bytes Tx
----------------------------------------------------------------------
vnet0      bridge vnet0    virtio  52:54:00:89:ab:cd 1.23 GiB 456.78 MiB
vnet1      user   --       virtio  52:54:00:7e:ab:cd 987.65 MiB 123.45 GiB








dominfo


Syntax:






dominfo domain






Display general information about a domain, including its
    state (running, paused, shut off), maximum memory, memory usage, number of
    vCPUs, and CPU time.






dommemadjust


Syntax:






dommemadjust domain --size size [--live] [--current]






Adjust the memory size of a domain. The --size option
    specifies the new memory size in mebibytes (MiB). The flags
    --live and --current control whether the change applies to the
    running guest or the persistent configuration.


This command allows for dynamic memory adjustments if supported by
    the hypervisor and guest OS.






dommemstat


Syntax:






dommemstat domain [--human]






Display memory statistics for a domain. This includes usage,
    major/minor page faults, swaps, and other memory-related metrics. The
    --human option displays sizes in human-readable format.






dommonitor


Syntax:






dommonitor domain






Connect to the QEMU monitor for a domain. This allows direct
    interaction with the hypervisor's monitor interface for advanced control and
    debugging.






domname


Syntax:






domname domain






Print the name of the specified domain. This is useful when
    you only know the domain's ID.






domnwfilterbind


Syntax:






domnwfilterbind domain --кновеfilter name






Bind a network filter (name) to a network interface of the
    specified domain. This allows applying network traffic filtering rules
    to specific virtual network interfaces.






dompmcaps


Syntax:






dompmcaps domain






Display the power management capabilities supported for a given
    domain. This includes information about suspend, hibernate, and
    live migration capabilities.






dompset


Syntax:






dompset domain --state state [--config] [--live] [--current]






Set the power management state of a domain. Valid states
    include suspend and hibernate. The flags
    --config, --live, and --current control persistence and
    runtime application of the state change.






dompu-memballoon


Syntax:






dompu-memballoon domain --state state






Set the state of the memory balloon device for a domain.
    The --state option can be zero (to deflate the balloon and
    release memory) or configured (to inflate the balloon to its
    configured maximum).


This command is used to dynamically manage memory allocation for
    running virtual machines.






domreboot


Syntax:






domreboot domain [--force]






Reboot a running domain. The --force option attempts an
    immediate reboot without cleanly shutting down the guest OS.






domrename


Syntax:






domrename domain newname






Rename a domain to newname. This changes the domain's
    name in libvirt's configuration.






domreset


Syntax:






domreset domain






Reset a running domain. This is equivalent to a hard reset or
    power cycle of the virtual machine.






domrestore


Syntax:






domrestore xmlfile [--running]






Restore a domain from a saved state file (XML). The
    xmlfile specifies the path to the saved domain XML file. The
    --running option attempts to restore the domain while it is running.






domsave


Syntax:






domsave domain [--destpath path]






Save the current state of a running domain to an XML file.
    The --destpath option specifies the output file path. If omitted, the
    state is saved to the default location for the domain.


This is useful for pausing a virtual machine and resuming it later,
    potentially on a different host.






domsendkey


Syntax:






domsendkey domain --key keycode --type type [--repeat count]






Send a virtual keyboard key event to a domain.
    keycode is the numeric key code, and type is the event type
    (press or release). The --repeat option
    specifies how many times to repeat the key event.


This is useful for sending special key combinations (like Ctrl+Alt+Del)
    to the guest OS.






domsetcpuid


Syntax:






domsetcpuid domain --leaf leaf --value value [--config] [--live] [--current]






Set a specific CPUID leaf value for a domain.
    --leaf specifies the CPUID leaf number, and --value provides the
    desired value. The flags --config, --live, and
    --current control persistence and runtime application.


This command is used for advanced CPU configuration, allowing fine-grained
    control over the CPU features exposed to the guest.






domsettlssecretpath


Syntax:






domsettlssecretpath domain --path path [--config] [--live] [--current]






Set the path to the TLS secret file for a domain. This is
    typically used for secure communication channels like SPICE. The flags
    --config, --live, and --current control persistence and
    runtime application.






domshutdown


Syntax:






domshutdown domain






Initiate a graceful shutdown of the guest operating system within a
    domain. This sends an ACPI shutdown signal to the guest.






domstart


Syntax:






domstart domain [--paused]






Start a stopped domain. The --paused option starts the
    domain in a paused state.






domstate


Syntax:






domstate domain






Display the current state of a domain (e.g., running,
    paused, shut off, blocked,
    inactive).






domstdin-action


Syntax:






domstdin-action domain --action action






Send an action to the standard input of a domain's console.
    Valid actions typically include send-break. This is used for
    interacting with specific guest OS consoles or virtual hardware.






domsuspend


Syntax:






domsuspend domain






Suspend a running domain. This saves its current state to
    memory and pauses execution. The domain can be resumed later using
    virsh continued.






domtime


Syntax:






domtime domain






Display the total CPU time consumed by a domain since its
    creation or last reset.






domtitle


Syntax:






domtitle domain






Get the title (or description) of a domain.






domto-cpu


Syntax:






domto-cpu domain --cpu cpu






Migrate a domain to a host with a compatible CPU specified by
    --cpu. This is a low-level command used in conjunction with migration
    operations.






domupdate-device


Syntax:






domupdate-device domain --device device-xml [--config] [--live] [--current]






Update a device within a domain's configuration using the
    provided device-xml definition. The flags --config,
    --live, and --current control persistence and runtime
    application.


This command is used for modifying existing devices, such as updating
    disk properties or network interface configurations.






domuuid


Syntax:






domuuid domain






Print the UUID of the specified domain. This is useful when
    you only know the domain's name or ID.






domvcpuinfo


Syntax:






domvcpuinfo domain






Display information about the virtual CPUs (vCPUs) assigned to a
    domain, including their state, emulator thread, and host CPU pinning.






domvcpupin


Syntax:






domvcpupin domain --vcpu vcpu --hostpin cpuset






Pin a specific virtual CPU (vcpu) of a domain to a set
    of host CPUs (cpuset). This allows for fine-grained control over CPU
    resource allocation and can improve performance by reducing context switching.


Use virsh help cpuset for information on the format of
    cpuset.






domxml-backup


Syntax:






domxml-backup domain --dest path [--current]






Create a backup of the XML configuration for a domain and
    save it to the specified path. The --current flag ensures that
    the live configuration is backed up.






domxml-destroy


Syntax:






domxml-destroy domain [--config] [--live] [--current]






Destroy (undefine) a domain's XML configuration. The flags
    --config, --live, and --current specify whether to remove
    the configuration from persistent storage, runtime, or both.


Note: This command only removes the configuration; it does not delete
    the disk images or other storage associated with the domain.






domxml-edit


Syntax:






domxml-edit domain --file path [--live] [--current]






Edit a domain's XML configuration using the specified file (path).
    This allows for bulk updates or complex changes to the domain's definition.
    The flags --live and --current control runtime application.






domxml-from-native


Syntax:






domxml-from-native domain --file path






Convert a domain's native configuration (e.g., from Xen or KVM's
    internal format) into libvirt XML format and save it to the specified file
    (path).






domxml-get-memory-tuning


Syntax:






domxml-get-memory-tuning domain [--live] [--current]






Retrieve memory tuning parameters (e.g., memory backing, hugepages)
    for a domain. The flags --live and --current specify
    whether to query runtime or persistent settings.






domxml-get-numa


Syntax:






domxml-get-numa domain [--live] [--current]






Retrieve NUMA (Non-Uniform Memory Access) configuration details for
    a domain. The flags --live and --current specify whether
    to query runtime or persistent settings.






domxml-get-vcpu-pinning


Syntax:






domxml-get-vcpu-pinning domain [--live] [--current]






Retrieve the vCPU pinning configuration for a domain. This
    shows which host CPU threads are assigned to each virtual CPU. The flags
    --live and --current specify whether to query runtime or
    persistent settings.






domxml-inactive


Syntax:






domxml-inactive domain






Print the XML definition of an inactive (shut off) domain.






domxml-list


Syntax:






domxml-list domain






Print the XML definition of a domain.






domxml-set-memory-tuning


Syntax:






domxml-set-memory-tuning domain --memorybacking path [--live] [--current]






Set memory tuning parameters for a domain, such as memory
    backing (e.g., using hugepages). The --memorybacking option specifies
    the path related to memory tuning configuration. The flags --live
    and --current control persistence and runtime application.






domxml-set-numa


Syntax:






domxml-set-numa domain --numa node-xml [--live] [--current]






Set NUMA configuration for a domain using the provided
    node-xml definition. The flags --live and --current
    control persistence and runtime application.






domxml-set-vcpu-pinning


Syntax:






domxml-set-vcpu-pinning domain --vcpu-pinningList cpusetList [--live] [--current]






Set the vCPU pinning configuration for a domain.
    cpusetList is a comma-separated list of host CPU sets, one for each
    vCPU. The flags --live and --current control persistence and
    runtime application.






domxml-to-native


Syntax:






domxml-to-native domain --file path






Convert a domain's libvirt XML configuration into a native format
    (e.g., for Xen or KVM) and save it to the specified file (path).






domxml-update


Syntax:






domxml-update domain --xpath expr --value value [--live] [--current]






Update a specific value within a domain's XML configuration using an
    XPath expression (expr). value is the new value to set. The
    flags --live and --current control persistence and runtime
    application.






host-cpu-baseline


Syntax:






host-cpu-baseline [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine]






Print the CPU model for the specified architecture that is the
    most compatible baseline CPU model available on the host. This is useful for
    finding a CPU model that will be compatible across many different host
    systems.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.






host-cpu-compare


Syntax:






host-cpu-compare [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine] [--baseline]
   {domain | file:/path/to/domain.xml}






Compare the CPU model of a domain (or a domain XML file) with the
    CPU models available on the host. This command provides detailed information
    about the compatibility of the domain's CPU model with the host.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.


The --baseline option compares the domain's CPU model
    against the baseline CPU model reported by
    host-cpu-baseline.


The first parameter can be a domain name or a path to a domain XML
    file prefixed with file:. If no domain or file is given, the command
    compares against the CPU models of the host.


The output indicates whether the domain's CPU model is fully
    compatible, partially compatible, or not compatible with the host's CPU
    features. It also lists any specific features that are available or missing.


This command is useful for troubleshooting CPU-related issues and
    for understanding the capabilities of virtual CPUs within the context of the
    host hardware and hypervisor configuration. It helps in determining if a
    domain will run correctly and perform optimally on the current host. It
    considers all CPUs of the host (even those not described in FILE).






host-cpu-models


Syntax:






host-cpu-models [--virttype virttype] [--emulator emulator]
   [--arch arch] [--machine machine] [--all]






Print the list of CPU models known by the hypervisor for the
    specified architecture. It is not guaranteed that a listed CPU will run on
    the host. To determine CPU model compatibility with the host, see
    virsh host-cpu-baseline and virsh host-cpu-compare.


The virttype option specifies the virtualization type
    (usable in the 'type' attribute of the <domain> top level element from
    the domain XML). emulator specifies the path to the emulator,
    arch specifies the CPU architecture, and machine specifies the
    machine type.


By default, only the models that are claimed to be
    "usable" by the hypervisor on the host are reported. The option
    --all will report every CPU model known to the hypervisor, including
    ones that are not supported on the hypervisor (e.g. newer generation
    models).








STORAGE COMMANDS


These commands manage storage pools and volumes.




pool-autostart


Syntax:






pool-autostart [--disable] pool






Configure a storage pool to be automatically started at each boot of
    the host. The option --disable disables the corresponding
    autostarting.






pool-define


Syntax:






pool-define xmlfile






Define a new storage pool based on the XML configuration in
    xmlfile.






pool-delete


Syntax:






pool-delete pool [--children]






Delete a storage pool. The --children option deletes all
    volumes within the pool before deleting the pool itself.






pool-destroy


Syntax:






pool-destroy pool






Destroy a storage pool. This stops the pool and makes it unavailable
    for use.






pool-edit


Syntax:






pool-edit pool






Edit the XML configuration of an existing storage pool using the
    default text editor.






pool-info


Syntax:






pool-info pool [--human]






Display information about a storage pool, including its state, type,
    total capacity, allocation, and available space. The --human option
    displays sizes in human-readable format.






pool-list


Syntax:






pool-list [--all]






List all defined storage pools. The --all option shows both
    active and inactive pools.






pool-redefine


Syntax:






pool-redefine pool xmlfile






Redefine an existing storage pool using the XML configuration in
    xmlfile.






pool-refresh


Syntax:






pool-refresh pool






Refresh the contents of a storage pool. This scans the pool for any
    new or removed volumes and updates libvirt's metadata.






pool-start


Syntax:






pool-start pool






Start a storage pool. This makes the pool and its volumes available
    for use by domains.






pool-state


Syntax:






pool-state pool






Display the current state of a storage pool (e.g., active,
    inactive, building).






pool-undefine


Syntax:






pool-undefine pool






Undefine a storage pool. This removes the pool's definition from
    libvirt's configuration. The pool must be inactive and empty.






vol-create


Syntax:






vol-create pool volxmlfile [--command-line]






Create a storage volume within a storage pool based on the XML
    definition in volxmlfile. The --command-line option allows
    specifying volume parameters directly on the command line.






vol-delete


Syntax:






vol-delete pool volume [--pool-delete]






Delete a storage volume from a storage pool. The --pool-delete
    option deletes the volume's contents and removes it from the pool.






vol-download


Syntax:






vol-download pool volume --file path [--offset offset] [--length length]






Download the contents of a storage volume to a file.
    pool is the storage pool name, and volume is the volume name.
    path is the destination file path. --offset and --length
    can be used to download specific parts of the volume.






vol-info


Syntax:






vol-info pool volume [--human]






Display information about a storage volume, including its name, key,
    capacity, allocation, format, and pool. The --human option displays
    sizes in human-readable format.






vol-key


Syntax:






vol-key pool volume






Print the key (unique identifier) of a storage volume.






vol-list


Syntax:






vol-list pool [--all]






List all storage volumes within a storage pool. The --all
    option shows volumes that might be hidden or inactive.






vol-path


Syntax:






vol-path pool volume






Print the path to the storage volume's data on the host.






vol-upload


Syntax:






vol-upload pool volume --file path [--offset offset] [--length length]






Upload the contents of a file to a storage volume.
    pool is the storage pool name, and volume is the volume name.
    path is the source file path. --offset and --length
    can be used to upload specific parts of the file to the volume.






vol-wipe


Syntax:






vol-wipe pool volume [--jobs number] [--granularity size]






Wipe the contents of a storage volume, securely erasing all data.
    pool is the storage pool name, and volume is the volume name.
    --jobs specifies the number of parallel jobs for wiping, and
    --granularity sets the size of data chunks to wipe.








NETWORK COMMANDS


These commands manage virtual networks.




net-create


Syntax:






net-create xmlfile






Create a new virtual network based on the XML configuration in
    xmlfile.






net-define


Syntax:






net-define xmlfile






Define a new virtual network based on the XML configuration in
    xmlfile. The network is not started automatically.






net-delete


Syntax:






net-delete network






Delete a virtual network definition. The network must be inactive.






net-destroy


Syntax:






net-destroy network






Destroy a virtual network. This stops the network and makes it
    unavailable.






net-edit


Syntax:






net-edit network






Edit the XML configuration of an existing virtual network using the
    default text editor.






net-info


Syntax:






net-info network






Display information about a virtual network, including its state,
    bridge name, network address, and MAC address range.






net-list


Syntax:






net-list [--all]






List all defined virtual networks. The --all option shows both
    active and inactive networks.






net-name


Syntax:






net-name uuid






Get the name of a virtual network associated with the given UUID.






net-start


Syntax:






net-start network






Start a virtual network. This makes the network and its resources
    available.






net-state


Syntax:






net-state network






Display the current state of a virtual network (e.g., active,
    inactive).






net-undefine


Syntax:






net-undefine network






Undefine a virtual network. This removes the network's definition from
    libvirt's configuration. The network must be inactive.






net-uuid


Syntax:






net-uuid network






Get the UUID of a virtual network.






net-xmldump


Syntax:






net-xmldump network






Print the XML definition of a virtual network.

secret-get-value secret-uuid

secret-undefine
